# cs.AI, cs.CV Daily Papers — 2026-11-16

## cs.CV

|Publish Date|Title|Authors|Abstract|PDF|

|**2026-11-16**|**ParseBench-2: Severity-Aware Evaluation of Document Parsing**|Pierre-Lo\"ic Doulcet et.al.|Document-parsing metrics can give high scores to outputs that change important facts and penalize changes that preserve meaning. People can often judge these consequences more easily than they can design a metric to measure them. We introduce a method for developing parsing metrics from severity policies that specify how each error type should affect the score. We express these policies through test scenarios that specify how a metric should behave. We then optimize the metric implementations against these fixed expectations, using coding agents to refine comparison rules, error detectors and weights under human review. This makes stated human priorities an explicit objective of metric design. We apply this method in ParseBench-2, a comprehensive document-parsing benchmark that combines broad capability coverage with metrics calibrated to error severity. It covers 4,317 pages across six dimensions of parsing quality: text, formatting, tables, charts, visual grounding, and forms. We use detailed annotations and targeted difficult cases to check for changed facts, broken structural relationships, and missing content. We take the lower of a parsing score that measures content recovery and a severity cap that penalizes detected errors, so recovering most of a page cannot hide a serious error. Across six severity test suites, our metrics more closely match the expert judgments expressed by the scenario targets than existing metrics and penalize at most 3.4% of the tested benign variations. We conduct a comprehensive evaluation spanning frontier vision-language models, specialized document parsers and open-weight systems, comparing their overall rankings, performance across parsing capabilities and tradeoffs between accuracy and cost.|[2611.17400](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17400)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Real-Time Diffusion Relighting as a Neural Sensor: Closing the Low-Light Domain Gap for Robotic Perception**|Shengjie Xu et.al.|Low-light conditions severely degrade robotic perception: object detectors trained on daylight imagery are prone to failure under dark and adverse weather conditions that fall outside of their training distribution. Furthermore, from an efficiency standpoint, existing Low-Light Image Enhancement (LLIE) methods are designed for offline processing and are therefore impractical to integrate into live control loops. To address these issues, we propose to embed a Generative Neural Sensor within the control loop that treats illumination as a generative prior rather than a signal processing problem, enabling task-relevant semantic recovery. We further propose an Real-Time Perceptual Architecture that decouples high-latency semantic recovery from low-latency robotic control. Rather than blocking the control loop, our system runs a large-scale image-to-image diffusion model in a background thread, and is activated only when a drop in detection confidence signals a perceptual uncertainty event, significantly reducing computational overhead. Our method sets a new state-of-the-art performance on the ExDark low-light benchmark with 87.1% mAP, and under adverse weather conditions, significantly boosts detection rates over competing methods. On a navigation task in a closed-loop autonomous driving simulator (CARLA), we achieve a 100% lap completion rate, establishing a simulation-based proof-of-concept for integrating high-latency generative models with standard robotic control autonomy stacks.|[2611.17438](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17438)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Adversarial Tracking: Attacks on Image Sensors for Object Tracking in Autonomous Vehicles**|Han Wu et.al.|Object tracking systems estimate the motion of surrounding vehicles by linking detected objects across video frames. In particular, Tracking-By-Detection (TBD) systems combine object detectors with motion prediction and data association to maintain object identities over time. However, neural network-based detectors are vulnerable to adversarial attacks that perturb input images, and their impact on downstream trajectory estimation is not fully characterized by detection accuracy alone. Therefore, this paper evaluates image-specific and image-agnostic attacks on YOLOv3 and YOLOv4 detectors paired with SORT, Deep SORT, Strong SORT, and OC-SORT tracking systems. The evaluation uses CARLA simulator and measures tracking quality through Multiple Object Tracking Accuracy (MOTA), Higher Order Tracking Accuracy (HOTA), and Identification F1 (IDF1). Under image-specific attacks, HOTA for YOLOv3-based trackers declines from 53.45-56.19% to 14.85-17.03%, while MOTA reaches -772.60% in the worst case. YOLOv4-based trackers exhibit greater robustness, while HOTA still declines to 25.06-26.86%. In addition, image-agnostic attacks can reduce the tracking accuracy using a single Universal Adversarial Perturbation (UAP), without generating a separate perturbation for individual frames.|[2611.17505](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17505)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Rethinking Visual Reasoning in Text-to-Image Reward Modeling**|Shiye Su et.al.|Reward models are a core component of modern text-to-image systems, serving as the objective for inference-time selection and RL-based post-training. A key prerequisite is visual understanding: to judge prompt adherence and ground aesthetic preferences, a reward model must bind attributes, resolve relations, and count reliably. We find that widely-used reward models perform poorly on targeted visual reasoning evaluations. Even when starting from strong vision-language backbones, preference fine-tuning erodes these capabilities, and continual-learning baselines fail to mitigate a steep tradeoff between visual reasoning and preference accuracy. We propose a data-centric intervention that injects visual reasoning supervision into preference learning by flipping the pairing axis. To complement conventional human preference data (paired images under a shared prompt), we convert existing visual reasoning datasets into preference pairs that share the same image but differ in prompt, and train on the mixture. This yields a Pareto improvement in both visual understanding and preference prediction. Across inference-time best-of-n selection and RL post-training, the resulting reward model improves text-to-image generation on challenging compositional prompts (GenEval) by an average of 4.0% and 5.5% respectively, outperforming HPSv3 which was trained on 6x more preference pairs.|[2611.17585](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17585)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Where to Predict in Retinal OCT? Anatomy-Guided Target Selection for I-JEPA**|Yunchu Feng et.al.|Self-supervised learning aims to learn meaningful representations from unlabeled data, potentially allowing models to capture subtle anatomical variations across patients and reduce the amount of labeled data needed. Image-based Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture (I-JEPA) learns by predicting target-region representations from visible context rather than reconstructing pixels. However, its uniform target placement does not account for the anatomical structure of retinal OCT, where retinal tissue occupies only part of each B-scan. We investigate anatomy-guided target selection using image intensity and predicted retinal segmentation while retaining the I-JEPA architecture and latent prediction objective. We compare anatomy-guided rectangular targets, anatomy-shaped targets and coverage-constrained placement with uniform masking, and evaluate the learned representations using linear probing for glaucoma classification on FairVision. We found that rectangular targets guided by image intensity achieved the highest linear-probe AUC in our comparisons. We show that anatomically meaningful target selection is promising for retinal OCT representation learning, while greater anatomical specificity did not consistently improve performance.|[2611.17586](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17586)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Training-Free Acceleration of Multi-View 3D Transformers via Regime-Aware Attention Approximation**|Muhammed Izzet Sezer et.al.|Multi-view 3D transformers such as VGGT reconstruct a scene from a set of unposed images in a single forward pass, predicting camera poses, depth and 3D points, but the cost of their global attention grows quadratically with the num- ber of images. Accelerators borrowed from large language models assume that each token attends to only a few oth- ers; on these models they lose accuracy or gain little speed. We study the global attention of three multi-view transform- ers on seven datasets and find that every layer plays one of three roles: it averages over the whole scene, matches points across many images, or anchors on the first image, whose coordinates the outputs use. Building on this anal- ysis, we propose Regime-AWare ATTention Approximation (RAWATTA), a training-free method that runs attention in 8- bit precision and keeps 16-bit precision only in the layers where a worst-case calibration check requires it. Its fast set- ting, RAWATTA-Fast, also approximates attention in a few averaging and anchoring layers, while matching layers and the first image keep all their keys. On a 12 GB GPU at 50 im- ages, RAWATTA runs VGGT faster than the released code, mostly through our CUDA engine, and loses at most about one point of pose accuracy (AUC@3) on any dataset; RAWATTA-Fast is slightly faster and less accurate. Both fit 200 images, where the released code runs out of memory at 100.|[2611.17629](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17629)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Provable Cross-Modal Transfer of Hierarchical Concept Structure in CLIP Embeddings**|Nghia Nguyen et.al.|Contrastive vision-language models such as CLIP are trained to align images and text, not to represent taxonomies. Yet empirical studies show that CLIP embeddings exhibit hierarchical structure aligned with the hierarchy of semantic concepts. A theoretical characterization of why such hierarchy emerges from a non-hierarchical objective remains open. In this paper, we formalize this phenomenon as Hierarchical Angular Separation (HAS), a geometric property whereby each non-leaf concept embedding is more similar to its descendants than to off-branch concepts. Empirically, we show that HAS holds approximately in raw images and randomly initialized CLIP image embeddings, while in text embeddings it holds approximately only after contrastive learning. To theoretically explain this asymmetry, we analyze an unconstrained features model in which the image embeddings are fixed and only the text embeddings are trained. At the global minimizer, the text-side HAS violation percentage at each node is at most the image-side HAS violation and near-violation percentage. The key mechanism is the hierarchical co-occurrence structure of image-text pairs, which biases text embeddings toward root-centered image prototypes. For a linear contrastive loss, we derive a closed-form solution that makes this mechanism explicit. We extend the analysis to the InfoNCE loss and establish analogous guarantees under sufficient regularization. Synthetic experiments validate the theory and show that HAS is transferred even beyond the regimes covered by our analysis.|[2611.17671](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17671)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Open-World Scene Deocclusion**|K. M. S. J. Kumarasinghe et.al.|Recovering the complete appearance of occluded objects from a single image is a fundamental problem for scene understanding and image editing. Real-world scenes are challenging due to clutter, complex spatial occlusions, and diverse object categories. Existing methods often struggle when occlusion involves background surfaces or objects that are not explicitly detected, and fail to resolve the cyclic occlusion dependencies that frequently occur in natural scenes. We introduce a training-free, open-world scene deocclusion framework that recovers the full amodal appearance of detected or user-selected objects from a single RGB image, decomposing the scene into depth-ordered RGBA layers that are geometrically plausible, semantically faithful, and visually consistent with the visible scene. Extensive experiments show that our approach improves scene-level deocclusion, producing coherent and realistic reconstructions in real-world settings. Code will be available on the project page: https://sashjk.github.io/owsd/|[2611.17695](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17695)|
|**2026-11-16**|**RaFL: An Empirical Study of Random Filter Losses for Image Reconstruction**|Ruibo Wang et.al.|Pixel losses train image reconstruction networks toward a point statistic of the target distribution, often averaging away fine textures under uncertainty. Perceptual losses alleviate this issue using pretrained features, but those features are inherently domain-specific. We study a random filter loss (RaFL) that compares prediction and target through filters with random weights, avoiding both pretrained features and task-specific loss engineering. RaFL combines random convolutions, random Fourier features, and random wavelet filters, automatically balancing their contributions through gradient-norm matching, eliminating manual loss-weight tuning and grid search. We systematically investigate three design choices: filter family, weight resampling schedule, and weight initialization. Based on validation PSNR and stability across random seeds, we select the configuration that uses all three filter families, redraws filters every optimization step, and employs Gaussian initialization. We evaluate RaFL on three 2D and two 3D image reconstruction tasks. RaFL outperforms a pixel loss on three tasks and matches it on two, and demonstrates stable performance across random seeds on all five tasks. RaFL adds less than 2% to the backbone's forward FLOPs and no inference cost, requiring $182\times$ fewer loss FLOPs than a VGG19 perceptual loss. These findings suggest that RaFL is a simple, tuning-free, and domain-agnostic loss objective for 2D and 3D image reconstruction.|[2611.17707](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17707)|
|**2026-11-16**|**QCAP-VQA: Question-Conditioned Capped Prior-Mass Supervision for Medical Visual Question Answering**|Yining Zhao et.al.|Medical visual question answering models can answer correctly without attending to the anatomy that the question names. Automatic region priors allow spatial supervision without expert masks, but they are noisy, and it is unclear how strongly they should shape attention and how their effect should be verified. We present QCAP-VQA, which supervises a subset of cross-attention heads with a question-conditioned prior built from ranked segmentation proposals. Its capped prior-mass objective penalizes insufficient attention inside the prior only below a threshold. We evaluate attention against SLAKE structure boxes that play no role in training, separating attention on the named anatomy from how attention shifts between questions about the same image. QCAP-VQA matches the accuracy of a baseline without spatial supervision (84.26% vs. 84.23%), and every evaluated prior-supervised model places more attention on the annotated structures. Over a 64-fold range of regularization strength, the capped objective keeps prior mass between 0.67 and 0.77, whereas a pooled region-mass objective drives it to 0.97 and loses accuracy. The cost lies in question conditioning: as the constraint widens or strengthens, attention shifts agree less with the expert-defined contrast. Across 13 configurations, prior-referenced and expert-referenced evaluation rank the models in largely opposite order (Spearman correlation of -0.87), and the capped models it favors track the prior's own errors. Because clinical use of medical visual question answering requires answers grounded in the queried anatomy, supervision from automatic priors should be judged against independent anatomical references.|[2611.17708](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17708)|
|**2026-11-16**|**HarmonyText: Harmonious Scene Text Editing via Reverse-to-Refined Pair Synthesis and Region-Decoupled Frequency Learning**|Ziqi Yi et.al.|Natural language-guided, mask-free scene text editing aims to modify text in real-world images using only textual instructions, yet remains challenged by unreliable pseudo-GT supervision, over-smoothed text generation, and the absence of GT-free evaluation. Existing methods often rely on imperfect pseudo-targets with background discrepancies, while standard MSE-based velocity regression tends to suppress high-frequency details, leading to blurred text boundaries and weakened local textures. To address these issues, we propose HarmonyText, a mask-free scene text editing framework with region- and frequency-aware supervision. HarmonyText introduces Region-Decoupling Optimization (RDO), which aligns edited text regions with pseudo-targets while anchoring preserved regions to the source image, and Cascaded High-Frequency Texture Guidance (CHFTG), which applies cascaded wavelet-domain constraints to preserve sharp boundaries and fine-grained textures of textual content. We further propose Reverse-to-Refined Pair Synthesis (RePair) to construct STE25K, a high-quality 25K paired dataset, and introduce a GT-free evaluation protocol with Color Distribution Divergence and a Harmony Assessment Model. Extensive experiments show that HarmonyText outperforms existing methods in text editing accuracy, background fidelity, color consistency, and perceptual harmony.|[2611.17733](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17733)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Stage-wise Lineage-aware Dropout for Sparse-View 3D Gaussian Splatting**|Yan Xing et.al.|Sparse-view 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) is prone to overfitting because only a limited number of images are available to constrain scene geometry. Dropout is one regularization strategy for alleviating this problem. We introduce the concept of a lineage family to describe a set of Gaussian primitives derived from the same founder through cloning or splitting operations. The founder Gaussians refer to all Gaussian primitives generated during initialization. Descendants within the same family often jointly contribute to the refinement of adjacent local regions. However, dropping individual Gaussians can be less effective due to the compensation effect from neighboring Gaussians for the dropped ones. To address this issue, we propose Stage-wise Lineage-aware Dropout(SLD), which applies lineage-family dropout during the structural regularization stage and switches to individual dropout during the later refinement stage. We further propose Lineage-Balanced Densification(LBD), which operates during densification by constructing an expanded candidate pool and reallocating the densification budget according to the mean gradient scores and existing descendant sizes of different families. Experiments on the LLFF, MipNeRF360, and Blender datasets show that the proposed framework achieves competitive performance in sparse-view reconstruction|[2611.17752](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17752)|
|**2026-11-16**|**VACO: Vertex-Aligned Decoding for Dense Hand Contact Estimation from a Single Image**|Binhong Ye et.al.|Dense hand contact estimation from a single RGB image is a foundational primitive for dexterous manipulation and 3D interaction analysis. Direct predictors typically decode the entire per-vertex contact map from one shared query, leaving individual vertices ungrounded in the image and smearing contact across adjacent, non-touching surfaces. We present VACO, a vertex-aligned decoder in which each MANO vertex samples local image features at its projected location. A hand-reconstruction backbone, jointly trained for reconstruction and contact, predicts these locations at inference without an auxiliary reconstructor. Training uses noise-perturbed ground-truth projections and end-to-end feature adaptation to improve robustness and contact-sensitive features. A single VACO checkpoint sets a new state of the art on five benchmarks spanning hand-object, hand-human, hand-scene, and bimanual interactions. It improves F1 by +20.7 pp over the strongest prior direct predictor on Hi4D and generalizes to the held-out HOT3D and HOCap, with gains of +5.5 and +2.7 pp. The resulting contact predictions also improve downstream HOI reconstruction.|[2611.17755](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17755)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Efficient Genotype Classification from 3D MRI in ADPKD via Density-Aware Point Sampling and E(3)-invariance**|Kwanghee Lee et.al.|Early detection of genetic kidney diseases, especially autosomal dominant polycystic kidney disease (ADPKD), is crucial because certain genotypes such as truncating PKD1 mutations cause rapid kidney growth and early progression to end-stage kidney disease. MRI-based genotype inference is a potential low-cost complement to genetic testing. However, although many deep learning methods have been developed for classifying various MR images, applying them to ADPKD remains challenging: (1) abdominal MR images are generally too large to be handled by deep learning models designed for smaller images like brain MRI, and (2) the amount of labeled data for ADPKD genetic mutations is insufficient to train deep learning models. In order to address these challenges, we propose E3PN, an efficient 3D MRI-based ADPKD genotype classification network that relies on point sampling and E(3)-invariance. We utilize a point representation of 3D MRI, which consumes less GPU memory than a voxel representation. For further memory efficiency, 3D points are sampled from a mixture distribution over the two kidneys in each image. Since E(3)-invariance has been shown to improve data efficiency, we also incorporate it into our model through coordinate canonicalization. On our ADPKD dataset, E3PN trains 163 times faster and uses 103x less GPU memory than a 3D ResNet, while also improving F1 from 0.165 to 0.226 over both baselines. These results establish a first benchmark for five-way ADPKD genotype classification from 3D MRI and show that point sampling and coordinate canonicalization yield two-orders-of-magnitude efficiency gains with a modest but consistent improvement in F1.|[2611.17758](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17758)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Creative Product Design under Brand Image Constraints**|Takeshi Onishi et.al.|Industrial design requires creative visual exploration while satisfying constraints that are often product-specific and implicit. Existing text-to-image (T2I) approaches can generate creative designs through concept blending or multi-agent self-enhancement, but they provide limited mechanisms for ensuring that generated designs remain consistent with such constraints. We focus on brand image as a representative design constraint and propose a framework for brand-aware redesign generation. Given a product name, the framework constructs a brand image survey report that summarizes product and market information, derives a creative direction from the report, and integrates this direction into a self-enhancement loop in which generated images are critiqued and prompts are revised. We evaluate the framework on 50 redesign tasks across five product categories, comparing it with Naive T2I, IP-Adapter/IT-Blender, and unconstrained self-enhancement under the same T2I backbone. By a VLM-as-Judge protocol to measure creativity and brand identifiability, the proposed framework achieves a more favorable trade-off between the two objectives, with particularly clear gains in highly constrained categories such as automotive and motorcycle, where the plausible design space is relatively limited compared with that of other product categories. These results suggest that dynamically eliciting and textualizing brand image can guide creative product design generation under brand image constraints.|[2611.17762](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17762)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Learning Anisotropic Prediction Targets for Flow Models**|Rayhan Zirvi et.al.|Flow-based generative models are trained on a hand-chosen prediction target, such as the velocity, the noise, or the clean data. This choice strongly affects training and sample quality. We introduce Anisotropic Flow Models (AnisoFlow), which replaces a fixed prediction target with a fully learnable one. AnisoFlow learns to scale the noise component of the target anisotropically, assigning a separate nonnegative weight to each direction in a jointly learned basis. A closed-form map recovers the velocity from the network's prediction, leaving the architecture, flow-matching loss, and sampler unchanged. Since recovery is exact, training optimizes the target purely to make it easier for the network to predict. We theoretically analyze the resulting prediction error and predict that the preferred noise weight in each direction scales near-linearly with data variance, in agreement with the weights learned empirically in pixel space. AnisoFlow improves FID from 1.90 to 1.55 over the JiT baseline on ImageNet 256$\times$256, by learning only the prediction target, without representation alignment or architectural changes. In pixel-space text-to-image generation, AnisoFlow improves image quality and, through better-conditioned velocity recovery, largely removes patch-seam artifacts under reduced-precision inference.|[2611.17767](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17767)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Towards Seconds-level Video Representation: Quadratically Gated Feature Grid with Adaptive, Prediction-free Sampling**|Tie-Yun Li et.al.|Implicit neural video representations are notoriously slow to build, which does not cross the basic practical threshold for most industrial applications. Here, we present Q-GAS, a coordinate-queryable video representation that couples multi-resolution explicit 3D feature grids with a compact SwiGLU-style quadratic-gating (QG) decoder and an error-aware, prediction-free sampler. The explicit grids avoid hash-collision coefficient sharing at the cost of modestly higher storage yet greatly boost the expressive capacity; the QG decoder introduces low-rank local second-order interactions to improve the nonlinear feature response. During fitting, a global error map periodically refreshes a high-error pool, and each batch mixes pool samples with uniform samples, which is free from the auxiliary model to predict difficult samples, thereby further escalating the efficiency. On UVG Beauty/Bosphorus/Jockey, Q-GAS fits and quantizes under 50 s on one RTX PRO 6000, decodes at over 370 FPS with mixed precision and about 146 FPS in FP32. In our PyTorch implementation, Q-GAS trains and quantizes 5.5-7.2 times faster than the evaluated Instant-NGP baseline and 19-28 times faster than RNeRV. Under a 90 s budget, it improves PSNR/MS-SSIM/LPIPS over the evaluated NeRV-family pipelines at comparable BPP over the measured overlap. We have shared our code at https://github.com/MillenRosen/Q-GAS for readers' free use.|[2611.17786](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17786)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Can Large Language Models Optimize Pretrained Neural Network Weights? A Multi-Phase Empirical Evaluation of Gradient-Free Editing in YOLO26 Object Detection**|Mohamed Abdallah Salem et.al.|Direct weight editing offers a gradient-free alternative to fine-tuning pretrained detectors, but its generalization and the value of large language model (LLM) guidance remain uncertain. We evaluate five experimental phases using YOLO26 models and MS COCO, comparing deterministic scaling, random perturbations, differential evolution, CMA-ES, structured interventions, real local-LLM proposals, failure-aware guidance, and hybrid LLM-CMA-ES against a matched random-policy control. Optimization uses native Ultralytics mAP50-95 on 1,000 COCO Val2017 images; selected candidates are assessed on a disjoint, previously examined 1,500-image subset, with COCO API AP50-95 reported separately where available. Increasing the CMA-ES budget from 8 to 250 evaluations raised development gains from +0.11 to +0.28 percentage points, while selection changes remained negative (-0.03 to -0.12 points); the 250-evaluation result uses one seed. The hybrid LLM approach showed no statistically established advantage over random-policy guidance (p = 0.612, 0.369, and 0.694 at budgets 8, 25, and 50). Five-seed YOLO26m replication yielded a COCO API AP difference of +0.004 points (95% CI: -0.026 to +0.034). Of 225 archived LLM proposals, 53 satisfy the final strict schema; early tensor-level execution cannot be fully verified. Genuine LLM-mediated weight editing is feasible, but the tested approaches did not demonstrate consistent transfer beyond the development subset or a measurable LLM advantage.|[2611.17789](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17789)|
|**2026-11-16**|**SceneAgent: A Multi-Agent Framework for Portable Game Scene Generation**|Xingyi Li et.al.|Moving a scene generator to a new game engine requires more than exporting its final output: planning, construction tools, and stored experience may also depend on the original backend. We present SceneAgent, a multi-agent framework for reusing the construction process and its experience across backends. A canonical SceneContext maintains the evolving world, while backend adapters isolate coordinate conventions, asset bindings, and export. Structured handoffs let fresh Builder sessions query this persistent state without inheriting the full construction transcript. Across generations, human-approved traces are distilled into asset-ID-free Layout skills and semantic Content skills that can be retrieved and recombined. We introduce GameSceneBench, comprising 69 indoor and outdoor prompts. Under a common asset and evaluation protocol, SceneAgent obtains human tie-adjusted preference scores of 78.2-89.4% against five baselines. Ablations show that reusable skills and the multi-agent protocol primarily improve prompt alignment and scene completeness, with additional runtime. Deployment in a second engine preserves the shared construction protocol and skill library while replacing backend integration components. Together, these results support separating persistent scene state, task-local reasoning, and reusable experience for portable scene construction.|[2611.17821](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17821)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Beyond Endpoints: B\'ezier Paths for Vision Foundation Model Adaptation**|Chenzhi Zheng et.al.|Frozen vision foundation models can be adapted by learning a few prompt tokens shared across all images. When prompts are updated as data shift or tasks change, the distribution of image features moves from one endpoint to another. Prior theory asks which endpoints prompts can reach; we ask whether they can travel between them along the shortest path. Because shared prompts compete with each image's own content for attention, they often cannot. We quantify this restriction as the extra transport cost that shared attention imposes over the shortest path. In an exactly solvable attention model, the reachable features form a cone; unrolling it into a plane gives the exact minimum action for every pair of endpoints and explicit optimal prompts on short arcs. The analysis reveals a sharp threshold in content normalization, which measures how much attention each image's own tokens absorb: for same-sign endpoints, at most two distinct levels allow prompts to follow the shortest path, whereas three or more force a strictly positive extra cost unless the endpoints differ only by rescaling. This gap persists with more prompts, small content perturbations, and aligned multihead outputs. On ViT-B/16 with VTAB-1k, a quadratic B\'ezier prompt path that minimizes feature action reduces held-out transport action relative to linear and cross-entropy-trained paths between the same endpoints and improves intermediate and online accuracy under unlabeled distribution shift, while cross-entropy-trained paths retain higher minimum accuracy when labeled calibration data are available. Reachable endpoints do not guarantee a reachable shortest path: how prompts move matters, not only where they end. Project page: https://xixiaouab.github.io/Beyond-Endpoints/.|[2611.17825](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17825)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Signal Scaffolding: Unlocking the Potential of Lightweight Models for Self-Supervised Monocular Depth Estimation**|Zhijie Wang et.al.|With the rising demand for edge devices, lightweight self-supervised monocular depth estimation methods have continued to evolve; however, existing methods generally suffer from poor accuracy. We find that the root cause may lies in the failure of conventional training regimes to fully exploit the inherent potential of lightweight models. To address this, we propose a novel three-phase training strategy for lightweight self-supervised monocular depth estimation, termed Signal Scaffolding (SS). Without introducing any additional dataset or parameters, we apply SS to ReForgeDepth, a lightweight network of our own design. Experiments on the KITTI and Cityscapes datasets demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance in the accuracy-efficiency trade-off and running in real time on edge devices. These results suggest that lightweight models can be improved further within their existing capacity, and that the central question may be less about how parameters are allocated than about how to unlock this latent potential through training.|[2611.17828](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17828)|
|**2026-11-16**|**V-JEPA-LM: Video-Language Models That Read Dynamics Beyond Static, Text-Aligned Representations**|Keuntae Kim et.al.|World-model video encoders such as V-JEPA learn how scenes change (direction, speed, order, state) from video alone. To answer questions, they are joined to a language model trained on captions and question-answer pairs. But each caption or question names only a few attributes of a clip, so the model learns to retrieve those attributes rather than to read the clip, and the dynamics the encoder carries go unused: on the same items, a linear probe reads speed from frozen V-JEPA features at 87% while the VLM answers at chance. We show where dynamics lives in a frozen encoder: in a subspace shared across videos for predictive encoders such as V-JEPA, but not as a shared component of frame-wise, text-aligned encoders such as SigLIP. We then build counterfactual supervision that makes the language model read it: pixel transformations turn public videos into answer-balanced variants whose answers are set by the transformation and differ only in the change. Trained in a single stage on 4.53M public samples with 32 visual tokens per frame, where open video LLMs use several stages, 9M to 172M samples and 64 to 256 tokens per frame on average, the resulting V-JEPA-LM models (1B to 8B, Qwen3 and Llama) lead every size class in average score across eleven video benchmarks; our 4B model scores 72.1, against 61.1 for the best open 4B and 64.8 for the best open 8B model. A controlled encoder x supervision study shows that the encoder decides what can be read and the supervision what is read: at a matched budget, counterfactual supervision adds 4.5 points on average and 38 on the speed questions of natural benchmarks. Code and models: https://github.com/kkt94/Llama-V-JEPA-LM.|[2611.17831](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17831)|
|**2026-11-16**|**PADSNet: Physics-Aware Dual-Stream Network with Biomechanical Priors for Laryngeal Lesion Classification**|Zhenzhen You et.al.|Precise classification of laryngeal lesions is clinically crucial yet highly challenging, as inter-class visual differences are often subtle. While abnormalities in vocal fold vibratory dynamics provide complementary discriminative information, existing deep learning models predominantly rely on visual appearance and lack explicit mechanisms for modeling clinically meaningful vibratory dynamics. To address this, we propose PADSNet, a Physics-Aware Dual-Stream Network for laryngeal lesion classification that integrates visual representations with explicit biomechanical reasoning. Specifically, a Wavelet-Attention Enhancement Module (WAEM) is designed to bridge the spectral-semantic gap, enabling complementary learning of spatial texture and dynamic frequency representations. Meanwhile, a Knowledge Computing Module (KCM) extracts interpretable biomechanical indices from temporal glottal distance waveforms, including indicators of mobility, symmetry, glottal closure, and vibration stability. These descriptors are subsequently incorporated into a Physics-Aware Gating Module (PAGM) as biomechanical priors to adaptively recalibrate visual feature toward pathology-relevant vibratory patterns. Extensive experiments on a clinical dataset demonstrate that PADSNet achieves an accuracy of 0.8452, an F1-score of 0.8090, a sensitivity of 0.7953, and a specificity of 0.8866. These results indicate that explicitly integrating biomechanical priors with deep visual representations provides a more accurate, robust, and clinically interpretable framework for automated laryngeal lesion classification.|[2611.17852](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17852)|
|**2026-11-16**|**DoubleTake: Focused Verification of Visual Evidence for Long-Document Understanding**|Kejun Xue et.al.|In long-document question answering, a vision-language model can answer incorrectly even when the retrieved pages contain sufficient evidence. Rereading those pages with the original question gives the model little guidance about which fact to check. We introduce DoubleTake, a training-free framework for focused verification of visual evidence. An initial reading produces a candidate answer and a separate evidence record that guides retrieval for a second reading. A planner compares the two candidates and their supporting evidence to formulate a factual question. A verifier answers it from the source images without seeing either candidate or evidence record, and a reasoner uses this observation to resolve the original question. With a frozen Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct backbone, DoubleTake achieves the highest overall accuracy among the evaluated methods on each of four benchmarks. With the same pages and number of model calls, asking a focused factual question yields higher accuracy on all four benchmarks than asking the original question again. Our code is available at https://github.com/small-code-cat/double_take.|[2611.17853](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17853)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Speed-Variation Distillation for Generative Modeling**|Sukit Seripanitkarn et.al.|While diffusion models excel at image generation, their denoising trajectories pass through corrupted intermediate states far from the image manifold. We ask whether generation can instead proceed through recognizable images throughout the entire trajectory. We introduce a generative framework built around a variation-reducing degradation process that contracts diverse images toward a shared prototype while preserving recognizable intermediate states. Because such manifold traversal lacks a simple analytical form, we construct the trajectories using generative-prior-based image morphing. We then distill the reverse process into a diversification operator that transforms the prototype into increasingly varied samples. To resolve the one-to-many ambiguity of the reverse process, we associate each trajectory with its initial noise latent and apply perturbations to improve robustness against accumulated errors. At inference, every step yields a recognizable image, allowing early termination and enabling a controllable speed--variation trade-off.|[2611.17868](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17868)|
|**2026-11-16**|**DySENSE: Dynamic Spatiotemporal ENergy Synthesis and Emulation with Satellite Imagery**|Kailai Sun et.al.|Cities account for over two-thirds of the world's energy and more than 70% of global carbon dioxide emissions. City energy is essential for achieving the 2050 net-zero target and the UN's Sustainable Development Goals 7 and 11. Although existing studies have made progress, they are limited by data and methodological gaps. Existing studies are largely predictive for existing cities rather than generative for future cities; fine-grained, aligned monthly dynamic energy data are scarce; and existing GenAI models struggle to generate dynamic urban functional monthly energy. We present DySENSE, a joint multimodal GenAI framework that synthesizes static urban satellite imagery together with spatially aligned, dynamic monthly building energy consumption maps. We decouple long-term static urban morphology from short-term dynamic energy consumption. By conditioning on static urban land use, DySENSE can generate realistic, diverse and new static urban imagery with aligned energy maps. By conditioning on dynamic monthly and climate prompts, DySENSE can jointly generate dynamic, monthly energy consumption maps while keeping the urban morphology static. Experiments across four cities (New York, Washington, Norfolk, Busan) demonstrated that DySENSE can generate annotated high-fidelity synthetic energy data using less than 25% labelled data, boosting downstream prediction performance by 8% mIoU, and achieving strong energy dynamic consistency. Compared to existing methods, DySENSE significantly improved prediction performance (8.7% accuracy) and reduced prediction error (9%-18% CVRMSE, ASHRAE). DySENSE offers an effective foundation for energy-efficient cities, peak-load analysis, building performance simulation and dynamic demand response for city, energy, and building science. The data and code: https://huggingface.co/datasets/skl24/MUSE2 and https://github.com/kailaisun/DySENSE|[2611.17878](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17878)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Photogrammetric 3D Model-Based Animal Behavior Classification**|Olaf Hellwich et.al.|Video-based behavior estimation is often implemented end-to-end from image sequences. We advocate a two-stage alternative: reconstruct 3D body and object geometry before classifying behavior. This makes ambiguities of the ill-posed 2D-to-3D inverse problem explicit and potentially correctable. We demonstrate the strategy with simulated interacting mice and extend it to real video.|[2611.17881](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17881)|
|**2026-11-16**|**See and Detect: Image-Driven Open-Vocabulary Multi-View 3D Object Detection**|Yating Xu et.al.|Recent open-vocabulary multi-view indoor 3D detection methods typically follow a point-cloud-driven pipeline, where pseudo 3D boxes are generated from reconstructed or estimated geometry and semantic labels are inferred from projected regions. However, such a design makes box generation dependent on the completeness of point clouds and restricts semantic learning to local appearance, causing inferior 3D object localization and open-vocabulary classification. In this paper, we present See and Detect, an image-driven framework that uses multi-view images as the primary evidence for 3D box and semantic label generation. For box generation, we first discover object candidates from individual images, enabling objects missing in point clouds to be recovered from visual evidence. However, single-view predictions are often noisy and redundant. To address this, we propose multi-view consensus box consolidation to fuse spatially consistent predictions across views, and box quality assessment to reduce the impact of boxes with partial object coverage. For semantic label generation, we introduce context-aware semantic learning, where semantic labels are queried from full image context, enabling more accurate semantic prediction. Extensive experiments on ScanNet and ARKitScenes demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art open-vocabulary multi-view 3D detection performance while running over 500$\times$ faster than the previous state-of-the-art method.|[2611.17897](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17897)|
|**2026-11-16**|**More Genes, Faster Inference: Efficient Large-Panel Spatial Transcriptomics Prediction from Histology**|Songming Zhang et.al.|Spatial transcriptomics (ST) prediction from histology aims to infer spatially resolved gene expression profiles directly from tissue images. This is a challenging problem that is currently often addressed with complicated model architectures, additional inputs, and complicated training procedures, while remaining limited to fixed, small gene panels. This raises a natural question: Is there an efficient method for large-panel ST prediction? Here, we present GeneFlow, a simpler yet effective framework for large-scale spatial gene expression prediction that unifies flow-matching generative modeling with structured multimodal fusion. Specifically, GeneFlow first encodes both histological morphology and cross-gene interactions in spatial spots through histology and gene tokenizers. The model directly predicts spatial gene expression, rather than an intermediate velocity field, while being trained with a velocity-based flow-matching objective. We show that, even without relying on heavy generative pipelines, GeneFlow achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple ST benchmarks, with up to 56.8% gains in 1,000-gene prediction. Extensive experiments further show that GeneFlow produces biologically meaningful spot profiles while reducing computational cost by orders of magnitude, thereby improving efficiency and scalability, particularly in clinical settings. We believe GeneFlow provides an opportunity for leveraging widely available histology images for downstream biological discovery. Code is available at: https://github.com/ZZhangsm/GeneFlow|[2611.17899](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17899)|
|**2026-11-16**|**AmbiSteer: Selectively Eliciting Ambiguity-Aware Responses in Vision-Language Models**|Monica Riedler et.al.|Vision-language models (VLMs) often exhibit overconfidence, providing firm answers even when visual evidence is insufficient. A prominent case is referential ambiguity in Visual Question Answering (VQA), where multiple entities in an image are plausible referents of a question. Addressing such cases requires recognizing when ambiguity is present: ambiguous questions can be handled by asking for clarification or enumerating plausible referents, while unambiguous questions should receive a direct answer. Current VLMs struggle with this distinction, often defaulting to a single interpretation. To investigate whether activation steering can serve as an inference-time mechanism to elicit such behaviors, we introduce AmbiSteer, a lightweight framework to selectively elicit ambiguity-aware behavior. Across three VLMs, we find that steering alone has limited and model-dependent effects, while prompting is generally more effective. Combining prompting and steering substantially increases clarification and enumeration behaviors, but also elicits them on unambiguous cases. AmbiSteer addresses this using a linear classifier to predict input ambiguity and condition the steering direction accordingly, increasing the proportion of inputs handled appropriately from 59.0% to 77.0% on average across models. Our work sets the ground for lightweight approaches to reduce model overconfidence in uncertain multimodal settings.|[2611.17900](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17900)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Zoom In, Scale Out: Metric Depth Models Mistake Digital Zoom for Moving Closer**|Rabeya Noor et.al.|Many new AI models estimate how far away things are, in metres, from a single photo. To do this they must also work out what camera took the photo. We check whether they really do with a simple test: digital zoom, that is, cropping a photo and enlarging the crop back to full size. Zooming moves nothing in the scene, so a correct model should give the same distance for every point still in the picture. The test is free, exact and needs no labels. Two numbers describe a model's reaction: its depth changes like $s^{-\alpha}$ (ideal $\alpha=0$) and its focal-length estimate like $s^{\beta}$ (ideal $\beta=1$), for zoom factor $s$. On 800 images from KITTI, NYUv2 and DIODE, the widely used Depth Anything V2 has $\alpha=0.95$: it treats a zoomed photo almost as if the camera had moved closer, because it judges distance mainly from how large things look. Models that also predict the camera remove only part of this error ($\alpha\approx0.31$-$0.36$), because their focal estimate grows too little. Given the true camera, Depth Pro and DA3-Nested keep their depth ($\alpha\approx0$), so the error lies in their camera estimate. The reason: a zoomed photo is, pixel for pixel, a normal photo of a scene squashed in depth, so size alone cannot tell the two apart, and a focal estimator trained by regression stays close to the cameras it saw in training. Undoing this needs no labels or training and makes Depth Pro pass the test ($\alpha=0.04$) at almost no cost on normal photos. Controls, including a real optical zoom lens, rule out blur and resizing artefacts. Finally, label-free fine-tuning for self-consistency lowers $\alpha$ to 0.05 on KITTI and 0.07 on NYUv2 once a "blurry means zoomed" shortcut is removed; we report what this costs on normal and unseen photos.|[2611.17903](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17903)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Cracks in Embeddings: Robustness and Stability of JEPA-Based Chest X-Ray Representations**|Zahid Mansur et.al.|Self-supervised Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) models are increasingly used for medical imaging, however there is a limited understanding about robustness of these models toward real-world image changes. We test four JEPA-based chest X-ray encoders, trained on 78,468 NIH ChestX-ray14 images, under six tests: two black-box adversarial attacks (SPSA, Boundary Attack), three image corruptions (Gaussian noise, brightness shift, contrast shift), and a label-scarcity test, measuring embedding change and classification AUROC. These perturbations cause severe, model-dependent damage: encoder-level Cosine Similarity drops as low as 0.33, and AUROC falls by as much as 0.12 in the worst case (I-JEPA under the Boundary Test). We test two ways of training the small predictor on top of each frozen encoder to address this. Adversarial fine-tuning, trained on clean and perturbed images together, raises embedding consistency above 0.97 in nearly every case and improves AUROC, relative to the same perturbed baseline, for most models, with the largest gain of $+0.10$ on I-JEPA. A Vector-Quantized codebook method, trained only on clean images and evaluated on perturbed test images for the first time, instead degrades under distortion for all four models, most noticeably for I-JEPA ($-0.15$ AUROC on average), showing that its discrete bottleneck alone does not provide the robustness that comes from training on perturbed data directly.|[2611.17905](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17905)|
|**2026-11-16**|**CardioTrace: Motion-Aware Cine Synthesis via Coupled Tracing and Segmentation**|Zhi Chen et.al.|Cardiac cine MRI provides time-resolved visualization of cardiac motion and is essential for evaluating ventricular function and anatomy. In routine clinical acquisition, however, cine sequences are frequently temporally sparse or degraded by arrhythmia, motion, and scan-time constraints, which can hinder reliable quantification and downstream analysis. We present CardioTrace, a motion-aware cine synthesis framework that generates a full cardiac cine sequence from limited input while explicitly modeling and tracing cardiac motion. CardioTrace couples two complementary pathways: a pixel-translation branch that directly predicts cine frames in a residual formulation, and a flow-field branch that estimates time-resolved deformation fields from a reference phase to provide interpretable motion correspondences across frames. To further preserve anatomical plausibility, a segmentation branch supervises clinically meaningful cardiac structures at key phases, and a cross-branch consistency objective encourages the synthesized appearance dynamics to follow the explicit motion guidance, mitigating temporal drift and unrealistic shape transitions over long sequences. The framework also supports prompt-style conditioning to incorporate auxiliary patient or acquisition information, improving robustness across heterogeneous multi-center data. Experiments on public cardiac cine MRI datasets demonstrate that CardioTrace improves perceptual quality and temporal coherence over representative GAN- and diffusion-based baselines, while better preserving ventricular morphology throughout the cardiac cycle. \textit{Our codes are available after review}.|[2611.17909](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17909)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Vessel2Brain: Edge-Aware Multi-Scale Flow Matching for 3D Brain Image Generation from Vessel Masks**|Zhi Chen et.al.|Generating realistic 3D brain MRI volumes from sparse anatomical priors is a highly ill-posed yet clinically valuable problem, with important applications in data augmentation, simulation, and downstream neuroimaging analysis. In this work, we investigate the challenging task of synthesising full 3D brain MRI volumes conditioned solely on vascular label maps. While vascular structures provide strong anatomical and topological cues, they occupy only a small fraction of the brain volume and offer no direct information about surrounding tissue appearance. To address this challenge, we propose Vessel2Brain, an edge-aware multi-scale conditional flow matching framework that explicitly incorporates vascular structure information into the generative process. Our approach formulates vessel-guided brain image synthesis as a conditional continuous flow, enabling stable training and flexible generation while preserving structural correspondence. A multi-scale flow matching strategy is introduced to jointly model global anatomical coherence and fine-grained local details, with auxiliary velocity predictions providing additional supervision across spatial resolutions. Furthermore, we design an edge-aware objective that emphasises vascular boundaries during training, encouraging anatomically faithful tissue synthesis around vessel structures. We evaluate Vessel2Brain on two public 3D brain MRI datasets with paired vascular annotations. Extensive quantitative and qualitative experiments demonstrate that our method consistently outperforms state-of-the-art generative models in terms of reconstruction accuracy, structural consistency, and distributional realism.|[2611.17919](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17919)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Token2Tile: Token-Wise Sparse Attention with Tile Execution through a Low-Rank Space for Video DiTs**|Xuecheng Wu et.al.|Attention is essential for modeling spatio-temporal dependencies in video diffusion transformers, but its quadratic cost makes it a major inference bottleneck. Its high sparsity offers an opportunity to accelerate generation by focusing computation on important interactions. However, accuracy and execution favor different granularities: each query attends to its own key tokens, whereas GPU kernels work on tiles. We present Token2Tile, a training-free sparse attention operator that combines per-query scoring and a per-query residual with tile execution. An online low-rank space, computed directly from queries and keys to preserve attention logits, makes clustering, scoring, and residual approximation efficient. Token2Tile unites query-specific selections to allocate exact work across query clusters, then packs the selected keys into GPU tiles. On MiniMax-H3 and Wan2.1-14B, Token2Tile delivers 1.48-1.54x end-to-end speedup over dense FlashAttention-3 with VBench scores within 0.5 points of dense. At comparable speeds on H3, it improves PSNR over SOL and SubBlock by 5.5-6.8 dB; on Wan, it exceeds SOL by 4.6-4.8 dB while running faster.|[2611.17930](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17930)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Domain Adaptation Based Pipeline for Character Classification and Handwritten Text Recognition**|Florent Imbert et.al.|Character classification and handwritten text recognition, particularly in specialized tasks, often suffer from limited labeled data, while a large amount of unlabeled data remains unused. To address this challenge, we propose a novel unsupervised domain adaptation-based pipeline to reduce the distribution gap between source and target domains. Our pipeline follows three main steps. In the first step, we pretrained an expert model on source data, to learn a strong general representation. In the second step, we perform unsupervised domain adaptation to leverage unlabeled target data, adapting the model to the specific characteristics of the target domain. In the third step, we trained the expert model on source data in the target style. By aligning the feature distributions between the source and target, our method enables the model to generalize effectively across diverse datasets. The unsupervised training increases the computation time only during training not in inference. We evaluate the pipeline on both character-level and historical handwritten line recognition tasks, demonstrating its flexibility. We establish a new state-of-the-art on the Safran-MNIST-DLS dataset defined in Track 2 of the DAGECC competition with 76.17% Macro F1-score and achieve competitive results on each of the specific datasets of READ 2018 without using the labels of the target data. Code is available at https://github.com/simon-corbi/DRANet-for-classification-and-htr|[2611.17952](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17952)|
|**2026-11-16**|**nnAEU: a Simple and Efficient CNN-Autoencoder for Hyperspectral Unmixing**|Edgard Dabier et.al.|Deep learning-based hyperspectral unmixing (HSU) has followed a trajectory of increasing architectural complexity, from simple autoencoders to transformers and, most recently, Mamba-based models, each claiming to advance the state of the art. Contrary to this trend, we propose a lightweight CNN-based autoencoder, coined "Not New Autoencoder Unmixing" (nnAEU). It is derived from the backbone of UNMamba (2025) through a pruning study that removes the Mamba component entirely. nnAEU uses less than 10 times fewer parameters than UNMamba, and far fewer than other transformer- and GAN-based methods, while outperforming them in accuracy. We provide a complete, minimal, reproducible code, available at https://gitlab.telecom-paris.fr/ring/nnAEU.git.|[2611.17956](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17956)|
|**2026-11-16**|**MacroGS: Macro-Pixel Shared Gaussian Primitives for Arbitrary-Scale Light Field Super-Resolutio**|Zeqiang Wei et.al.|Arbitrary-scale light field spatial super-resolution reconstructs high-resolution views at any requested scale from low-resolution multi-view observations. Gaussian primitives enable continuous reconstruction, but generating separate primitive sets for each view redundantly encodes shared scene structure across views and causes the primitive count grow linearly with the number of views. To avoid such redundancy, we propose MacroGS, a feed-forward decoder that jointly predicts a single shared Gaussian set per macro pixel rather than generating separate sets for each view. Disparity-dependent replacement and angle-conditioned appearance allow each set to be reused across all output views, keeping the predicted primitive count independent of the number of views at each requested scale. The decoder iteratively predicts per-primitive disparities, retrieves the corresponding multi-view features, and updates the primitive states. Gaussian splatting and residual refinement then reconstruct the output light field. Despite being learned without geometric supervision, the predicted disparities strongly correlate with underlying scene geometry. Experiments across five benchmarks demonstrate that MacroGS integrates effectively with diverse light field encoders, improving reconstruction quality at fixed scales while achieving leading performance across most evaluated arbitrary-scale settings. The decoder also achieves speedups of approximately $2$-$10\times$ over state-of-the-art implicit light field decoders and $10$-$18\times$ over the view-wise Gaussian baseline.|[2611.17957](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17957)|
|**2026-11-16**|**From Images to Grounded Edits: A Real-World Benchmark for Chinese Grammatical Error Correction in Handwritten Essays**|Weiru Zhang et.al.|Chinese Grammatical Error Correction (CGEC) is typically formulated as a text-to-text problem. While suitable for digital texts, this formulation falls short in real-world K-12 education, where AI-assisted corrections are expected directly on essay images, which are primarily handwritten and exhibit many irregularities. To bridge this gap, we introduce Grounded Chinese Grammatical Error Correction (G-CGEC), an image-to-grounded-edit task requiring systems to predict both corrections and their positions on essay images. We propose G-CGEC-Eval, a benchmark comprising real-world images of K-12 Chinese essays with fine-grained annotations. For evaluation, we adapt IoU-based metrics from object detection and propose a more tolerant alternative based on center-distance matching. Using this benchmark, we evaluate open-source and proprietary vision-language models under a direct single-pass setting, as well as settings with auxiliary components (OCR and character grounding) coordinated via pipelining or agentic harnessing. Results show that even top-tier proprietary models perform surprisingly poorly in the single-pass setting, while pipelining and agentic harnessing yield meaningful improvements. Notably, an error-type-based multi-agent workflow achieves the best performance, establishing it as the strongest baseline for future G-CGEC research. Through ablations and fine-grained analysis, we find the task remains far from solved, with linguistic error discovery and character-level grounding as the primary bottlenecks. This underscores the need for models that better integrate visual perception with linguistic reasoning and visual grounding.|[2611.17966](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17966)|
|**2026-11-16**|**RecurIR: Learning When and How to Revise Restoration Policies via Recursive Self-Improvement**|Hu Gao et.al.|Agent-based image restoration has achieved promising performance by coordinating restoration tools to handle heterogeneous and coupled degradations. However, existing agents often improve their decisions through accumulated successful and failed executions, without explicitly identifying the conditions under which learned restoration strategies remain effective. Consequently, changes in degradation severity, spatial coverage, or interactions may invalidate previous decisions, while providing limited guidance on how these decisions should be revised. In this paper, we propose \textbf{RecurIR}, a recursive self-improvement framework that enables restoration agents to learn when and how to revise their decision rules. Specifically, we introduce a policy-hypothesis discovery mechanism to identify testable, condition-dependent rules through matched tool executions. We then design a rule-falsification-driven task evolution mechanism to generate targeted degradation variations, examine the validity of learned rules, and identify their applicability boundaries. Based on the resulting evidence, we develop a change-conditioned policy revision mechanism that learns how variations in degradation conditions should modify tool selection, execution order, operation strength, and processing regions. We further incorporate independent verification to retain effective revisions and use the resulting feedback to guide subsequent hypothesis discovery and task evolution. Through this recursive process, RecurIR progressively refines its restoration policies as degradation conditions change. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework.|[2611.18006](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18006)|
|**2026-11-16**|**RSSA: Robust Semantic and Spatial Aligner for Collaborative Perception**|Zhihao Yang et.al.|V2X collaborative perception improves single-vehicle perception by aggregating multi-view features from collaborative agents. However, existing methods typically rely on simple spatial warping to align the collaborative features, which can cause semantic misalignment during fusion. Meanwhile, these deterministic warping-based methods are highly sensitive to the positional noises. In this paper, we propose a plug-in module called Robust Semantic and Spatial Aligner (RSSA), which performs semantic and probabilistic spatial transformations for robust feature alignment. Specifically, a Semantic Transformation (SemT) block modulates feature channels based on the observed relative pose to correct semantic inconsistencies. Subsequently, a Probabilistic Spatial Transformation (P-SpaT) block performs probabilistic warping by sampling from the Gaussian-modeled relative pose space. A Relative Transformation Augmentation (RT-Aug) strategy is further introduced to augment the relative poses and facilitate the training of the RSSA. Extensive experiments on both simulated and real-world datasets demonstrate that RSSA improves the state-of-the-art methods by a large margin under the configuration of both clean and noisy positions.|[2611.18023](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18023)|
|**2026-11-16**|**JaRS: Contrast-Free Localization of Memorized Regions in Diffusion Models via Jacobian Row Sums**|Saisuchith Mahajan et.al.|Diffusion models sometimes memorize and reproduce regions of their training images, raising privacy and copyright concerns. We aim to localize these regions. Existing contrast-based methods use the diagonal of the denoiser Jacobian, which reflects the posterior variance of each pixel. However, a small diagonal entry is ambiguous: it signals either memorization or averaging over many neighboring pixels in smooth or predictable regions. To resolve this ambiguity, these methods subtract a reference map, which requires a second evaluation with a null prompt or an earlier checkpoint. Unconditional models have no null prompt, and earlier checkpoints are unavailable in general. We show that Jacobian row sums (JaRS), unlike the diagonal, separate memorization from local averaging without a reference map. Pairing JaRS with an image-level detection score that uses the same statistic at an early denoising step yields JaRS+. Across Stable Diffusion v1.4 and v2.1, Realistic Vision v5.1, and an unconditional diffusion Transformer (DiT), JaRS+ outperforms contrast-based localization methods.|[2611.18024](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18024)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Multi-level latent-diffusion adaptation for cross-region point cloud segmentation of ancient terraces**|Sina Mohammadi et.al.|Automated lidar feature detection from lidar point clouds can support archaeological surveys, but models trained in one landscape may transfer poorly to another because of differences in terrain, vegetation, acquisition characteristics, and feature morphology. We address this problem for cross-region detection of ancient terraces using transductive unsupervised domain adaptation that exploits an unlabelled target survey. We propose MELD (Multi-level Embedding-space Latent Diffusion), which trains separate diffusion denoisers on target-domain representations at four hierarchical PointNet++ feature levels. These target distribution priors are then used to refine source representations during supervised training and target representations during inference, allowing adaptation to act directly on the multi-scale features consumed by the segmentation decoder. We additionally investigate local geometric descriptors derived from K-nearest-neighbour neighbourhoods, including surface normals, slope, and aspect. MELD is evaluated bidirectionally between the Teyuna and Miraflores lidar surveys in the Sierra Nevada de Santa Marta, Colombia, against a source-only model and nine configurations of established domain adaptation approaches. MELD raises F1 over the source-only model from 0.397 to 0.459 for Teyuna-to-Miraflores transfer and from 0.557 to 0.594 in the reverse direction, achieving the highest F1 among the evaluated adaptation methods in both directions. Adding local geometry further raises F1 to 0.542 and 0.606, respectively. Multi-level ablation shows that refining all four PointNet++ levels provides the strongest performance in both directions. These results show that explicit modelling of target feature distributions in hierarchical point cloud feature space, complemented by local geometry, provides an effective route to cross-region archaeological mapping without target labels.|[2611.18033](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18033)|
|**2026-11-16**|**InfiHall-Bench: A Benchmark for Hallucination Evaluation in Autoregressive Text-to-Image Generation**|Yuxia Qiao et.al.|Hallucination benchmarks for text-to-image generation are predominantly designed around diffusion models, limiting their diagnostic value for visual autoregressive (VAR) models and the development of mitigation methods. We identify two limitations in existing prompt construction paradigms: many prompts are no longer challenging for modern generators, while category-based construction relies on recurring object-attribute-relation templates and rarely tests interactions among multiple visual requirements within a single image. We introduce InfiHall-Bench, a controlled benchmark of 411 prompts constructed by systematically composing five foundational and visually verifiable skills: attribute binding, spatial relation, action relation, part consistency, and scene consistency. Each prompt covers 4.05 annotated skills on average, enabling the evaluation of hallucinations under diverse combinations of visual requirements. Using this benchmark, we find that hallucinations in VAR models exhibit a scale-wise persistence pattern: semantic errors can emerge at different autoregressive scales and are rarely corrected by subsequent fine-scale steps. Motivated by this observation, we propose Confidence-Masked Hallucination Mitigation (CMHM), which estimates the prediction confidence of target visual tokens at each autoregressive scale during teacher-forced fine-tuning. CMHM downweights reliably predicted tokens, allowing optimization to focus on uncertain predictions across scales. Experiments show that InfiHall-Bench distinguishes models that existing compositional benchmarks rate as comparable, while CMHM consistently outperforms uniform LoRA fine-tuning for VAR-based text-to-image generation.|[2611.18053](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18053)|
|**2026-11-16**|**SWAM: Saliency-Weighted Asymmetric Merging for Efficient Vision Transformers**|Utkarsh Kumar Singh et.al.|Vision Transformers (ViTs) achieve strong performance on image recognition tasks, yet their quadratic self-attention complexity over input tokens creates prohibitive computational costs for practical deployment. Token reduction methods offer a practical solution by progressively reducing the token sequence during the forward pass. We propose Saliency-Weighted Asymmetric Merging (SWAM), a training-based token merging framework that extends the Bipartite Soft Matching (BSM) with two saliency-aware mechanisms. We introduce a two-step matching formulation in which cosine similarity decides candidate pairs, while a multiplicative maximum saliency penalty regulates their selection for merging. We then further use asymmetric exponential saliency-weighted aggregation, which allows more salient tokens to contribute more strongly to the merged representation. A lightweight MLP-based saliency prediction module (75k parameters per stage, 1% of backbone parameters) supervised via attention rollout is trained end-to-end jointly with a DeiT-Small backbone using a composite cross-entropy, knowledge-distillation, and saliency-alignment objective. On CIFAR-100, TinyImageNet, and ImageNet-100, under a conservative schedule that reduces tokens from 196 to 100 patch tokens, with the CLS token retained throughout, 27.5% FLOPs reduction and 1.35 times speedup, SWAM matches or exceeds baseline across the reported results. Under an aggressive schedule (196 to 70 tokens with the CLS token kept throughout, 33.8% FLOPs reduction, and 1.46 times speedup), accuracy gains are achieved on ImageNet-100 (+0.23%) with minimal losses on CIFAR-100 and TinyImageNet.|[2611.18057](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18057)|
|**2026-11-16**|**TelePhysica: Agentic Reconstruction of Physically Interactive Scenes**|Ruiqi Shu et.al.|Direct image-to-video diffusion models entangle pixel synthesis with implicit latent motion, precluding persistent 3D mechanical equilibrium, mid-rollout intervention, and counterfactual force control. Conversely, single-pass programmatic 3D reconstruction from an uncalibrated photograph leaves monocular depth and support clearance underconstrained, causing reconstructed scenes to interpenetrate or collapse under gravity. We present TelePhysica, a closed-loop framework that reconstructs an executable, multi-regime physical world from a single photograph to drive stateful 3D interaction and trajectory-grounded video synthesis. A vision-language coding agent synthesizes separable visual meshes and declarative physical models within a two-stage verification loop that pairs pixel-aligned silhouette critique with a zero-force gravitational equilibrium preflight. The verified world compiles into a unified solver coupling rigid bodies, articulated chains, co-dimensional cloth shells, and 3D tetrahedral elastodynamics, whose simulated rollouts condition pretrained video diffusion models through pixel-aligned structural streams. On a 100-scene single-image benchmark, closed-loop execution raises equilibrium-verified world recovery from $0\%$ in single-pass synthesis to $81\%$. Controlled constitutive ablations show over an order-of-magnitude larger localized corner displacement and out-of-plane pleating in finite-element cloth than in matched rigid-plate proxies. Conditioning video diffusion on simulated trajectories increases dynamic motion-mask overlap by $2{\times}\text{--}5{\times}$ and preserves motion direction under counterfactual force reversals where direct image-to-video models fail.|[2611.18078](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18078)|
|**2026-11-16**|**AG-JEPA: Anatomically Guided Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture for Neurodegenerative Representation Learning**|Azad Singh et.al.|Brain MRI can reveal early signs of Alzheimers disease (AD) before measurable cognitive decline becomes apparent. However, during the transition from cognitively normal (CN) to mild cognitive impairment (MCI), the disease-related signals are often subtle and confined to only a few brain regions. Existing Joint Embedding Predictive Architecture (JEPA) models learn general structural representations but do not explicitly capture the anatomical regions affected in AD. We propose Anatomically Guided JEPA (AG-JEPA), which introduces anatomical tokens that interact with patch tokens during pretraining to learn region-specific representations from brain regions relevant to neurodegeneration. Building on this representation space, we further propose Prog-AG-JEPA, which models disease progression trajectory as a conditioned latent displacement within this space. We evaluate AG-JEPA across five downstream tasks spanning Alzheimer's disease diagnosis and progression prediction. AG-JEPA improves diagnosis and MCI prognosis, achieving an 8.4 percentage point AUROC gain over the strongest baseline on CN/MCI classification and a 7.1 percentage point gain on out-of-distribution CN/AD classification on the MR-RATE dataset, highlighting its effectiveness across both early-stage detection and cross-cohort generalization. For disease progression modeling, Prog-AG-JEPA consistently outperforms all baselines on future diagnosis and brain volume change prediction, demonstrating the value of anatomically grounded neurodegeneration representations for diagnosis and prognosis.|[2611.18097](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18097)|
|**2026-11-16**|**EcoVGGT: Pinned-Key Inference for Efficient and Streamable Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction from Long Videos**|Hongsen Cao et.al.|Feed-forward multi-view transformers such as VGGT recover the cameras and geometry of a video in one pass, but the cost of their cross-frame attention grows quadratically with the frame count, and their accuracy drops on long videos. Most remedies approximate attention over all frames within a budget or select a few keys by their content. We hypothesize that the keys of a long video can be chosen without looking at its content, and our experiments on VGGT and $\pi^3$ support it: a few dozen evenly spread frames can serve as the keys of every frame. We present EcoVGGT, a training-free inference framework whose keys form a Pinned Key Set of 31 such frames. A pin pass stores their tokens in a Scene Cache, and the other frames read it in independent batches that reproduce joint inference with these keys exactly, at a constant cost per frame. An Online Stream updates the pinned frames during recording. Six middle global blocks align the frames and form a reusable VGGT module: the Alignment Score reads their attention to flag failures, and Alignment Refocusing reduces them by fine-tuning only these blocks (3.6 GPU hours). EcoVGGT ($\pi^3$) matches the success rate of $\pi^3$ on 1,000-frame ScanNet videos at 13.8 times its speed. With refocusing, EcoVGGT (VGGT) raises the success rate of VGGT from 70% to 82% at 8.5 times its speed on 500 frames. EcoVGGT reconstructs 4,000 frames on one GPU, and EcoVGGT ($\pi^3$) streams 1,000 frames at 18.6 frames per second with the lowest online absolute trajectory error.|[2611.18099](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18099)|
|**2026-11-16**|**KnowVis: Dual-View Evaluation and Diagnosis of World-Knowledge Grounding in Text-to-Image Models**|Yonghang Yu et.al.|Text-to-image (T2I) models have made substantial progress in visual realism, aesthetic quality, and instruction following. However, real-world prompts often go beyond explicit visual descriptions and require implicit facts, structured knowledge, and domain-specific commonsense. Existing evaluations mainly focus on explicit prompt-to-image semantic alignment or specific knowledge subdomains, lacking unified evaluation and diagnosis of world-knowledge grounding in T2I models. We introduce KnowVis for dual-view evaluation and diagnosis of world-knowledge grounding in T2I generation. Built on a hierarchical knowledge taxonomy with case-level annotations, KnowVis combines two complementary benchmark views with a structured MLLM-based VQA judge protocol. The Skill-Tree Benchmark supports fine-grained atomic knowledge evaluation, while the Generalized Benchmark examines open-ended knowledge composition and expression. The protocol uses Required VQAs to assess atomic knowledge correctness in the Skill-Tree Benchmark, and combines Required, Optional, and dynamically discovered knowledge expressions with human-judge collaborative evaluation in the Generalized Benchmark. We validate the protocol's reliability on the Skill-Tree validation split, enabling scalable evaluation and diagnostic error attribution. Experiments show that KnowVis reveals notable gaps in world-knowledge grounding across existing T2I models and provides structured diagnostic signals for knowledge-intensive image generation.|[2611.18101](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18101)|
|**2026-11-16**|**DRA-HTR: Disentangling Representation and Adaptation for Handwritten Text Recognition**|Gayan H. Pathirage et.al.|Handwritten text recognition (HTR), particularly when applied to historical documents, remains challenging due to the scarcity of annotated data and the substantial domain shifts caused by variations in handwriting styles, document degradation, acquisition conditions, and linguistic characteristics.
