Restoring the corresponding execution provenance recovers 85.5% of transport failures, while recovery is lower when provenance is lossy or unavailable. These results show that native-view accuracy alone is insufficient to characterize verifier reliability when execution evidence crosses abstraction boundaries.|[2611.18471](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18471)|
|**2026-11-16**|**From AI-Enabled Remote Patient Monitoring Toward Agentic Robotics: Embodiment, Governance, and Care Integration**|Ivana Ragusa et.al.|AI-enabled remote patient monitoring is evolving beyond passive data collection toward agentic AI systems that interpret patient states, coordinate care, and initiate action across domestic and clinical settings. This structured scoping review finds that mature agentic care has not yet emerged: Most systems remain human led, while stronger decision-action coupling and embodied AI remain largely confined to prototypes and pilots. Healthcare robotics makes the transition from monitoring to action especially visible by translating AI-generated recommendations and decisions into speech, movement, prompting, physical assistance, escalation, and care coordination, making embodiment, clinical integration, human oversight, and governance fundamental design requirements. Further progress requires safe and interoperable care architectures in which intelligent components operate through clinically validated and accountable workflows. We propose agentic robotics as a future direction for supervised systems embedded in the care ecosystem, connecting AI agents and robots with clinicians, caregivers, and patients through auditable workflows that can be interrupted and remain subject to human oversight and explicit AI governance.|[2611.18484](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18484)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Interactive Program Induction with Language Model Priors**|Robin Shing Moon Chan et.al.|Program induction refers to the process in which a learner aims to recover a program from a few given input-output examples. This paradigm broadens programming access by letting users demonstrate the rule-based behavior they want to apply to unseen data, instead of having to implement it themselves. However, recovering a program from finite input-output examples is fundamentally ambiguous, as a finite set of input-output examples is theoretically consistent with infinitely many programs whose output diverges on unseen inputs. We view this problem from a Bayesian perspective, where, given a prior $p^0$ over programs, identifying the target program means concentrating posterior probability mass on it by observing additional input-output examples. In the interactive program induction setting, the learner chooses maximally informative inputs and queries an oracle, such as the user, for their outputs. We derive a greedy query-selection algorithm and show that it terminates after $\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{\gamma}\log 1/p^0(f^\star))$ informative oracle queries, and returns the target program $f^\star$ up to approximate equivalence when no other class outweighs it under the prior. The algorithm uses a language model as the prior under a PAC-style relaxation of program equivalence, where the query-selection objective is estimated using importance sampling. On the CodeARC benchmark, the algorithm improves accuracy over the LM only agent by up to 22 percentage points while observing up to 10.5 fewer input-output examples.|[2611.18485](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18485)|
|**2026-11-16**|**TradeGauntlet: Evaluating Compliance Controls for Autonomous Trading Agents**|Sandip Nandi et.al.|Autonomous trading agents need controls that prevent problematic orders without making legitimate tasks impractical. We study this aspect of agentic compliance with TradeGauntlet, a replayable limit-order-book simulator that separates detection, prevention, and execution cost. Gates eliminated those alerts by rejecting self-crossing orders, while the aggregate-gate condition reduced Sonnet's two-sided fills by about 15%. Scripted probes and two search methods test weaknesses beyond those observed in the LLM runs. The searches find split-order schedules that evade the implemented conduct rules; a learned baseline catches all 2,400 tested searched episodes but also flags 15% of a large maker's sessions. Aggregate caps reduce the response-dependent gain unevenly, with smaller reductions when only near-touch depth affects prices. A response fitted to five Nasdaq stocks and a separate ABIDES experiment expose the dependence of these estimates on market assumptions. A distinct adversarial prompt elicited refusals from Sonnet in all 240 rounds, yielding no candidate strategies to evaluate. TradeGauntlet provides reproducible tests of controls around trading agents; the experiments do not establish legal compliance or real-market detection accuracy.|[2611.18493](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18493)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Agentic AI for Discovering Fault-Tolerant Quantum Circuits**|Aritra Lahiri et.al.|Scaling quantum computing toward practical quantum advantage is currently limited by the efficiency of circuits that operate on information encoded by quantum error-correcting codes. The full stack of quantum computing encompasses several tasks: preparation of logical code states, implementation of logical gates, logical routines on encoded qubits, etc. Yet, these circuits are largely designed by hand for individual cases, with some recent works incorporating machine learning for certain aspects. We formulate this problem as agentic evolutionary program synthesis, wherein a large language model, leveraging its pretraining knowledge and tool-use proficiency, and guided by a circuit verifier, evolves a program that defines a Clifford circuit for a wide range of curated tasks such as logical state preparation, logical gate design, and logical routines such as code-switching, code growth, etc. Our approach consistently finds fault-tolerant circuits for all of these tasks, each verified by an exhaustive check over faults.|[2611.18504](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18504)|
|**2026-11-16**|**MeReader: An Offline, Privacy-Preserving, Progress-Aware AI Assistant for Narrative eBook Reading**|Mohammed Efaz et.al.|This paper introduces MeReader, a privacy-preserving, offline and progress-aware AI assistant designed to function as a contextual companion for narrative reading on consumer hardware. Unlike conventional Large Language Model (LLM)-based systems that process documents/books online in an all-texts-at-once type of way, thereby risking narrative spoilers, MeReader confines both retrieval and generation to the reader's distinct position in the text. We evaluate the system across classical English books using both quantitative comprehension metrics and qualitative LLM-based independent judging. The results identify a quality-latency frontier, showing that mid-sized local models (2b-4b) can achieve comprehension parity with larger counterparts (5b+) while maintaining acceptable response quality. Importantly, the proposed progress-aware boundary mechanism is shown to prevent future content leakage without compromising retrieval quality and correctness. These findings suggest that a localized, privacy-centric approach can augment, rather than replace, the digital reading experience, offering a good alternative to cloud-dependent services. Therein, there remains a lack of amalgamate systems which combine progress-aware, privacy-preserving, and narrative eBook reading with AI support. It is in this very gap that the development of MeReader came to be; synthesizing said strands into an offline, book-constrained assistant focused on enhancing comprehension without compromising narrative continuity or user privacy.|[2611.18513](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18513)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Elliptic Sampling: Optimal Interpolant Schedules for Gaussians and Beyond**|Cole Becker et.al.|Generative modeling based on stochastic interpolants requires specifying two key choices: an interpolant schedule bridging the initial and target distributions, and an efficient discretization of the resulting probability-flow ODE. In this work, we introduce a new framework for joint schedule/discretization design based on the analyticity properties of the (complexified) ODE trajectories, which in turn ensure good numerical behavior. Pairing an elliptic schedule with a tailored discretization, we obtain Zoloflow, a new sampling algorithm closely connected to Zolotarev's optimal rational function approximants. Crucially, both the elliptic schedule and Zoloflow explicitly incorporate the condition number $\kappa$ of the target distribution and reduce to the classical trig schedule in the limit $\kappa \rightarrow 1$. |**2026-11-16**|**WeightBench: Evaluating Language Models as Weight-Space Programmers**|Aayush Marasini et.al.|Recent work shows that large language models (LLMs) can operate directly on the parameters of other neural networks. We introduce WEIGHTBENCH, a benchmark that evaluates LLMs of increasing capability on 42 WSP tasks across five tracks: reading, fixing and writing weights, recognizing edits that are impossible, and data-free compression. |**2026-11-16**|**From Chats to Markets: AgenticPay for LLM-Powered Negotiation in Multi-Agent Commerce**|Xianyang Liu et.al.|Large language model agents are increasingly expected to negotiate and transact autonomously, yet existing benchmarks rarely combine multimodal product grounding, multi-issue bargaining, and competitive multi-agent markets. AgenticPay includes 160 multimodal tasks across four business scenarios (E-commerce, Food Delivery, Ride-hailing, and Apartment Rental) and eight market topologies, from bilateral bargaining to many-to-many competition. Agents interpret product images, infer opponents' hidden preferences, and negotiate binding contract terms such as price, lease duration, return policy, and delivery speed through multi-round natural-language dialogue. A utility-based framework evaluates agreement quality and joint welfare. Experiments with proprietary (GPT-5.4, Claude Sonnet 4.6, Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview) and open-weight (Qwen3-VL-32B-Instruct, InternVL3-38B) multimodal models reveal substantial limitations in value creation and market reasoning. As markets shift from bilateral to multi-sided, average GlobalScore falls by 5.7 points, with open-weight models declining by up to 11.5 points. |**2026-11-16**|**Evaluating Chain-of-Thought Reasoning through Reusability and Verifiability**|Shashank Aggarwal et.al.|AI applications increasingly compose multiple models, where one model acts on the output produced by another model. The transferred text can also carry the reasoning of the sender model. Chain-of-thought is our experimental instance of such text. We ask three questions about such inter-model reasoning transfer. Is the reasoning transmitted reliably? Is it useful to the receiver? Is it safe? We study these three questions with a Sender-Receiver setup. A Sender LLM produces a reasoning trace. A separate Receiver LLM must act on it. Neither model knows the identity of the other. The Sender therefore cannot tailor its reasoning to a particular Receiver. Reliability asks whether the Receiver recovers the Sender's intended conclusion. We measure it with Verifiability. Usefulness asks whether transferred reasoning helps a Receiver correct its mistakes. We measure it with Correctability. Safety asks whether transferred reasoning makes a Receiver abandon a correct answer and follow the wrong reasoning supplied by the Sender. We measure it with Deceptiveness. All three factors (Task, Sender, and Receiver) affect all three metrics that we measure. However, there are some interesting trends. Nature of task is a more important factor in the reliability of reasoning transfer. Reliability is higher on arithmetic tasks and lower on science and commonsense tasks. The nature of Receiver plays more important role while deciding the usefulness of reasoning transfer. The nature of the Sender matters most for whether corrupted reasoning harms the Receiver.|[2602.17544](http://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17544)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Recursive Belief Vision Language Action Models**|Vaidehi Bagaria et.al.|Vision-language-action models must enable agents to execute long-horizon tasks under partial observability. However, most existing approaches remain observation-driven, relying on short context windows or repeated queries to vision-language models (VLMs). This leads to loss of task progress, action repetition under perceptual aliasing, and high inference latency. While semantic grounding is important, long-horizon manipulation fundamentally requires persistent, action-conditioned state representations. |**2026-11-16**|**Uncertainty Quantification for Multimodal Large Language Models with Incoherence-adjusted Semantic Volume**|Gregory Kang Ruey Lau et.al.|Despite their capabilities, Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) may produce plausible but erroneous outputs, hindering reliable deployment. |**2026-11-16**|**Multi-Environment POMDPs with Finite-Horizon Objectives**|L\'eonard Brice et.al.|Partially Observable Markov Decision Processes (POMDPs) are systems in which one agent interacts with a stochastic environment, and receives only partial information about the current state. In a multi-environment POMDP (MEPOMDP), the initial state is unknown, and assumed to be adversarially chosen. In this work we focus on computing the optimal value and policy in MEPOMDPs with finite-horizon objectives. That problem is known to be PSPACE-complete in POMDPs. Our main results are as follows: (1) we establish that it is also PSPACE-complete in the more general setting of MEPOMDPs; (2) we present a practical algorithm and evaluate it on classical benchmarks, significantly outperforming the only previously known algorithm.|[2605.07537](http://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07537)|
|**2026-11-16**|**Do LLMs Experience an Internal Polylogue? Investigating Reasoning through the Lens of Personas**|Nils A. Herrmann et.al.|Recent work shows that large language models (LLMs) encode behavioural traits ("personas") as linear directions in activation space, often called "persona vectors". Prior work has used such directions as static handles for behavioural steering. We instead treat them as dynamic signals: probes we can monitor and intervene on as reasoning unfolds. Experiments across four open-weight models show that polylogue features predict correctness on MMLU-Pro on par with low-dimensional activation baselines, while remaining interpretable through their associated persona directions. They also suggest concrete steering targets, namely which latent directions to modulate at different stages of a response. We instantiate this as a simple paragraph-conditioned intervention that improves accuracy on three of four models but degrades the fourth, suggesting that stage-aware latent steering is possible but not yet robust. Together, this positions the polylogue as an interpretable tool for reasoning-time monitoring and intervention.|[2605.09159](http://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09159)|
|**2026-11-16**|**D-VLA: A High-Concurrency Distributed Asynchronous Reinforcement Learning Framework for Vision-Language-Action Models**|Yucheng Guo et.al.|The rapid evolution of Embodied AI has enabled Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models to excel in multimodal perception and task execution. However, applying Reinforcement Learning (RL) to these massive models in large-scale distributed environments faces severe systemic bottlenecks, primarily due to the resource conflict between high-fidelity physical simulation and the intensive VRAM/bandwidth demands of deep learning. This conflict often leaves overall throughput constrained by execution-phase inefficiencies. To address these challenges, we propose D-VLA, a high-concurrency, low-latency distributed RL framework for large-scale embodied foundation models. D-VLA introduces "Plane Decoupling," physically isolating high-frequency training data from low-frequency weight control to eliminate interference between simulation and optimization. We further design a four-thread asynchronous "Swimlane" pipeline, enabling full parallel overlap of sampling, inference, gradient computation, and parameter distribution. Additionally, a dual-pool VRAM management model and topology-aware replication resolve memory fragmentation and optimize communication efficiency. |**2026-11-16**|**ShopGym: An Integrated Framework for Realistic Simulation and Scalable Benchmarking of E-Commerce Web Agents**|Chinmay Savadikar et.al.|Developing and evaluating e-commerce web agents requires environments that preserve meaningful task structure while enabling controllable, reproducible, and scalable scientific comparison. Its simulation layer, ShopArena, converts live seed storefronts into self-contained sandbox shops through anonymized shop specifications and a staged, validated generation process. |**2026-11-16**|**Certified Policy Optimisation for Nested Causal Bandits via PAC-Bayes Risk**|Tim Woydt et.al.|Critical sequential decisions are rarely single-timescale: a strategic decision causally shapes the context in which every subsequent tactical choice is made; standard bandit and reinforcement-learning theory does not capture this causal coupling between timescales. Our main theoretical result is a causal PAC-Bayesian excess-risk bound that certifies any candidate deployment policy from historic data alone, off-policy and anytime, answering the deployment question: can we trust this agent here, and at what risk? Experiments on a hierarchical SCM show that, against a matched RFF-GP joint regression on the same function class, the factorised SCM-mechanism posterior transfers significantly better zero-shot under exogenous distribution shifts, the recursive meta-to-inner commit significantly dominates the joint-commit alternative in distribution, and the certificate significantly contracts as offline data accumulates. Combining these results, we establish progressive certified handover, a safe-deployment method: each timescale flips from a legacy controller to NCTS when gains can be certified, independently of the others.|[2605.29788](http://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29788)|
|**2026-11-16**|**CoffeeBench: Benchmarking Long-Horizon LLM Agents in Heterogeneous Multi-Agent Economies**|Issa Sugiura et.al.|LLM agents are increasingly capable of handling complex, long-horizon tasks and may eventually take on business management, a role traditionally performed by humans. Deploying them safely requires monitoring their capabilities and behavior in environments that reflect real economies. Six autonomous LLM coding agents run its farmers, roasters, and retailers, and negotiate and trade with one another to maximize their own net income over 90 simulated days. We evaluate eight recent proprietary and open-weight models by letting each run one roaster while a fixed background model runs the other five firms, and scoring that roaster's net income. Claude Sonnet 5.5 earns the highest net income, closely followed by GPT-6 Astra. The leaders sell at higher prices, and the best model also buys its inputs far more cheaply, whereas weaker models trade at thin margins. Since performance targets are a common cause of accounting fraud in human firms, we also test whether agents under KPI pressure inflate their numbers. Under a revenue target, circular trades occur for some pairs of models: one agent proposes that the two rival firms sell goods back and forth at inflated prices so that both book revenue, and the rival accepts. We hope CoffeeBench serves as an early step toward a future in which LLM agents manage firms without misconduct. |**2026-11-16**|**Hallucination-Free GUI Grounding via Regression-Free Layout-Aware Matching**|Yuke Li et.al.|GUI agents are shifting from metadata-dependent large language models to purely visual multimodal large language models (MLLMs) that operate directly on screenshots. |**2026-11-16**|**ReForge: Keeping ABR Algorithms Never Finished with Verified Large Language Model Revisions**|Zhiqiang He et.al.|Designing an ABR algorithm for a network scenario takes an engineer months, and large language models (LLMs) now do this work in hours. |**2026-11-16**|**Programmable Distributions of Semantics-Preserving Variants for Evaluating Code Agents**|Hasan Najib Mahmud et.al.|Code agents are increasingly deployed to resolve real software issues, yet their reliability under superficial code variations remains poorly understood. We evaluate whether coding agents that repair repository-level issues remain reliable when the surrounding codebase is rewritten into a semantically equivalent form. We sample semantically equivalent variants of a repository from an explicitly defined distribution and compare the agent's resolve rate on them with that on the original. |**2026-11-16**|**From Regulation to Implementation: A Critical Evaluation of LLM-Assisted Regulatory Compliance in Industry**|Marco B\"ucheler et.al.|The European Union (EU) has emerged as a leading regulatory body in the development of sustainability and privacy regulations. Creating these compliance artifacts, however, is challenging. Industrial data, which often exists in heterogeneous formats and is scattered across company and supplier systems, is required for DPPs and can be difficult to extract into compliant DPP formatting. Furthermore, DPIA documents require interdisciplinary expertise and follow no standardized format, making development difficult for novel systems. To address the particular complexity of compliance artifact creation for both regulations, researchers have proposed the use of LLMs in the generation process; however, the impact of the aforementioned problems on the output of these systems is largely unaddressed. This work investigates the existing research gap by exploring how data extraction instructions and regulatory vagueness impact the quality and consistency of LLM-produced compliance artifacts. The resulting artifacts are evaluated by benchmarking different models against manually created ground-truth schemas. |**2026-11-16**|**AI Agents Push Humans Out of the Loop**|Margaret Mitchell et.al.|AI agents pose significant risks as they are granted increasing autonomy. A commonly proposed solution is human oversight and keeping a ''human in the loop'', but this is not a simple solution: Not only do current approaches to AI agent design impede effective human oversight, but the cognitive capacities required for it are also themselves degraded by extended use of AI systems. This position paper argues that current approaches to the development and deployment of AI agent systems do not support effective human oversight -- they contribute to its degradation. To address this, a top priority in the advancement of AI agents should be supporting the situated goals and cognitive requirements of effective human oversight, treating the human needs of overseers at the same level of importance as AI agent capability. To put this idea into practice, we connect work on automation and human-computer interaction to AI agent processes, outlining design-level affordances and organizational protocols that (1) support overseers in exercising critical judgement and (2) counteract the skill atrophy that arises from extended use of automation. |**2026-11-16**|**Efficient Test-Time Adaptation through Human-Agent Interaction**|Zora Zhiruo Wang et.al.|AI agents are trained on population-scale data to encode broad capabilities spanning those of many practitioners. Yet the artifacts they produce rarely meet the personal bar professionals need to stake their reputation on. |**2026-11-16**|**MeanFlowAdvantage: Stable Reward Fine-Tuning for Few-Step Average-Velocity Generators**|Haocheng Tang et.al.|MeanFlow enables efficient few-step generation by predicting interval-average velocities, but this representation creates a mismatch for reward fine-tuning: existing advantage-based objectives are typically defined on instantaneous velocities or equivalent $x_0$-space predictions, whereas inference directly uses the learned average-velocity map. |**2026-11-16**|**Proof-Grounded Patient-Specific Clinical Explanations from Knowledge-Graph Reasoning**|Surajit Das et.al.|Clinical decision support can lack auditable links from observations and encoded knowledge to conclusions and recommendations. Across six usable workbooks (11,289 patients, 33,867 patient-disease traces, 1,715,928 feature-evidence rows), IG-range validity, knowledge provenance, cross-sheet numerical fidelity (203,202/203,202), and exported fact/report field presence (33,867/33,867) reached 100.00%. Observed zero-activation and partial instances numbered 111,488 and 226,880, respectively. A separate seeded 30-patient end-to-end audit ran failure-free, passing all prespecified trace, licensing, provenance, and state-consistency checks. Paired comparisons (90 traces, fixed inherited diagnostic scores and policy catalogue) showed the availability-only baseline changed every report but no action sets. |**2026-11-16**|**When to Rethink: Learning Multi-Perspective Self-Verification for Vision-Language Models**|Ziquan Zhu et.al.|Vision-language models (VLMs) have achieved strong performance in multimodal reasoning, yet they remain prone to generating plausible but incorrect answers. |**2026-11-16**|**Self-Building Labs**|Jiaru Bai et.al.|Self-driving laboratories (SDLs) accelerate scientific discovery by automating experimental execution and optimization. |**2026-11-16**|**TorchTPU - A Hybrid Eager/Compiled Architecture for Native PyTorch Execution on TPU**|Claudio Basile et.al.|TorchTPU is an open-source collaboration between Google and Meta that introduces a native and principled PyTorch backend for Google TPUs. |**2026-11-16**|**FlashRT-PAI: Fast Kernels Are Not Fast Pipelines for Physical AI**|Liang Su et.al.|Vision-language-action (VLA), video-action (VA), and world action models (WAMs) form the backbone of modern physical AI systems, yet their real-time single-batch inference remains a defining bottleneck. |**2026-11-16**|**Pluralistic Alignment in Large Language Models: Objectives, Human References, and Preference Representations**|Meng-Chen Wu et.al.|Pluralistic alignment aims to represent meaningful variation in human preferences rather than collapse heterogeneous judgments into a single target. |**2026-11-16**|**TimesJev: Thinking Fast on Time Series with System 1 Sufficiency**|Chenghao Liu et.al.|Large language models (LLMs) have recently shown strong potential for time series reasoning, while models that reason over time series are usually with long chains of thought. |**2026-11-16**|**Can LLMs Improve Their Own Harness? A Controlled Study on ARC-AGI-3**|Jens Lehmann et.al.|A generally intelligent system acquires new skills efficiently without requiring humans to redesign its software scaffold for every new task. |**2026-11-16**|**One Offset, Two Directions: Baseline Adjustment via Subgroup Offsets**|Jiaying Zhou et.al.|Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) estimates advantages against the within-prompt mean reward, a baseline shared across the group. In practice, rollouts within a prompt are not always homogeneous, differing in their solution strategies and reasoning trajectories, along which they may group into subgroups. We further find that these heterogeneous subgroups may exhibit distinct reward distributions. The global baseline, however, collapses them onto a single credit axis, where the credit reflects neither a rollout's standing within its subgroup nor the subgroup's standing relative to the rest. We therefore study how to adjust the baseline when the rollouts of a prompt fall into heterogeneous subgroups. We define the \emph{subgroup offset} as the deviation of a subgroup mean from the global mean, and shift the baseline along it by a single signed coefficient $\lambda$. The sign of $\lambda$ yields two opposing directions: \emph{Alignment} ($\lambda{>}0$) adds the offset to the baseline, drawing credit toward a rollout's relative position within its subgroup; \emph{Contrast} ($\lambda{<}0$) subtracts it, drawing a rollout's credit toward the relative quality of the subgroup it belongs to.
